It supersedes, for this note, the provisional ledger (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N1-claims-ledger.md@6485da2), which stays as committed because README-NOTE.md and the N12 scrub index cite it. Sections 1-8 are the provisional round's block-quotation ledger, re-run here by the same code at the same pins (record bbc9ffe, item raws 09c3a8c); section 7 is now a summary of the matcher over the three files, and section 8's notes 1 and 6 are updated. **Section 9 is new: one row for every numeral unit in the note and both SVGs**, read from the verified mapping `results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N1-note-rows.json` (sha256 `5f8f76cd5b4e18bff19c9419044734eff3aab22bce86d005438fd56e2e01f6ce`) and validated mechanically at this run. Section 10 is the values check of round 5 (the final checker's findings and the PI's N19 routing, with the mutation self-test); sections 11 and 12 are rounds 6 and 7 (the adversarial check of round 5 and the strict check of round 6). 10f, what the validator still does not catch, is current at this run.

## 1. Extraction: every numeral the block quotes (block lines 21-133)

Rule. block lines 21-133 read at the record pin; a numeral token is a maximal digit run with internal '.' or ',' groups (regex \d+(?:[.,]\d+)*|\.\d+) or a number word (zero..twelve, half; case-insensitive, word-bounded). Each token is classified in this order: inside a hand-mapped ledger row's span -> that row; inside a hand-declared exclusion span -> its category; else a generic rule (backticks, date, hash, arXiv/figure locator, file name or path, section sign, digits attached to a name, link/card/tier/stage/level/item/version prefix, one-/two-sided); else UNASSIGNED.

Count: **506 numeral tokens** in block lines 21-133 (471 digit tokens, 35 number words). Classified:

| class | tokens |
|---|---|
| excluded (generic) | 211 |
| excluded (hand) | 39 |
| row (identifier inside a row span) | 20 |
| row (note-figure numeral) | 236 |

Ledger rows: 166 (hand-mapped, each a span of one block line). Hand exclusion spans: 31. Unassigned: **0**.

Numeral-free rows. Every numeral token above is assigned by the rule. A span without a numeral gets a row by this rule: a numeral-free span gets a row when the block lists it among the figures to be reproduced for the note: an entry of an item's figure list, a qualifier or definition attached to quoted figures, an N3(ii)-(v) result, or a figure label; requests for an account, a method, a provenance or a table get none (examples in numeral_free_no_row: Code's read of block lines 21-133, not an exhaustive enumeration). Examples with rows: 'zero pauses' (L101), 'the three conjuncts' (L108), 'the leakage gate's bound' (L146), F1's link-5 y-axis label (L081), and, added in round 2 after the values check, 'the random-direction comparison' (L162, :43), N3(v)'s random-direction control (L163, :48), the definition of Δ (L164, :57), 'the falsifier' (L165, :104) and 'the purity–potency figures' (L166, :107). Rows L001-L161 keep their round-1 ids; L162-L166 sit in block-line order among them.

'Identifier inside a row span' counts digits that sit inside a row's span but are names (L60, random-1, F1, link-5, Horn-1); they belong to the row's wording, not its value, and --note matching ignores them. A decimal after 'word-' is a quantity, not a name (round 2: 'sub-0.05-cosine' in L161 counts as a figure).

## 2. The ledger

One row per figure as the block quotes it. 'item raw row' is the item raw's own line at the audit pin. 'printed', 'source' and 'status' are the item raw's, verbatim (long values are cut at 400 characters here; the JSON carries them whole). 'entry (record)' = the latest commit, over the row's record sources, at which a source entered the record (artifact: last change at or before the pin; line locator: the blamed commit); 'entry (+audit)' adds the audit raw (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-*) where the figure's only source is an audit raw (computed-new; source-only). A '(cont.)' line is a second primary item-raw row for the same figure; a '(see)' line carries N1's note on the row, the primary item-raw row's own note where the status cell does not already carry it (round 3; cut at 700 characters, whole in the JSON), and related item-raw rows, each with its own status (shown, not used in the pass check).

The block (AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:68, AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:70, AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:107, AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:137) and README.md (README.md@bbc9ffe:58) say 103/103. The suite at --ref prints 154/154; the committed site check says results/raw/2026-10-07_m-dispositions_sitecheck.txt@bbc9ffe:10 '154/154 reproduced through valence_core'. The pass condition applied here is Code's reading, not a ruling by the design seat or the PI: the block's preamble (AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:3) says that where a referent is wrong, Code names the right one in the raw and proceeds, and Code's preflight raw (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-preflight.md@bbc9ffe:198-199) reads N11 as passing if the suite prints N/N from a fresh clone, with N stated in the raw. That reading is flagged for the design seat and the PI. Because N/N alone would also pass a clone that silently skips checks whose artifact is absent, conjunct (e) is added. Every tracked line that says 103/103 is listed in (3) with its kind (live doc, the block, dated record entry); only the live doc and the block make a present-tense claim about the count. README.md is not edited by this audit; the PI decides its wording, and the wording of the README-NOTE the block's §6 item (3) describes. Lineage of the count, from (2e): at 0dd6f56 (the commit git blame names for README.md@bbc9ffe:58's 103/103, which also wrote RESULTS.md@bbc9ffe:11593) a fresh clone prints 103/103 (exit 0). Of those 103 checks, 100 are at --ref with identical expected, got and cited source; 0 are at --ref with a different remainder; 3 are not at --ref ('S2: grounded panel needs no new extraction'; 'S2: vignette is marked ungrounded'; 'S2: an ungrounded axis cannot decide the reading'), each last changed by e1b5c77 (git log -S), whose subject begins 'S2 adjudications applied: vignette grounded and approved verbatim, persona settled on the ...'. Of --ref's 154 checks, the other 54 are later additions, 8 of them first added by that same commit ('S2: the panel is all three neighbours'; 'S2: two neighbours need no new extraction'; 'S2: the vignette is the one that must be built'; 'S2: vignette is grounded by adjudication'; "S2: construct name of record carries 'Ho-style' retired"; 'S2: persona slot settled on the character reading'; 'S2: an unadjudicated axis cannot decide the reading'; 'S2: the approved vignette DOES decide the reading'). So --ref's 154 checks are not a strict superset of the 103; for README-NOTE item (3), 'what 103/103 covers'. | | | | | | | |

Blame: every line locator's entry commit is `git blame -L n,n --porcelain <rev> -- <file>`, run per line (244 distinct (file, rev, line) entries over the primary and related rows' sources and the note locators); each is cross-checked against one full-file `git blame --porcelain` of its file: 244/244 equal.

## 4. The block's pass check (block :23)

*N1 final:* 4a-4g are the provisional round's pass check over the block's quotations, kept as built. Where they say the note is not on this machine or defer to the --note run, section 9 is that run: 9d is the pass check over the note's own figures, 9e the watch-list per conditional STOP, and 9f the span notes that read the 4g corrections against the note's sentences.

*Pass:* every figure is `reproduced` or `log-only` with a locator. *On fail:* a `not found` figure is cut from the note; a `discrepant` figure is corrected in the note to the reproduced value if the discrepancy is transcription, and is a STOP on that item if it is not. (AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:23)

Over the 166 provisional rows (176 primary item-raw rows; a row with two primary rows passes only if both do): primary-row statuses {"reproduced": 124, "discrepant": 16, "log-only": 8, "computed-new": 19, "not found": 3, "other": 3, "mixed": 1, "not run": 2}.

- Rows whose every primary row is reproduced or log-only: 123 of 166; 123 of them give a pinned line locator for every log-only primary row.
- Passing rows that an item raw ties to a wording, scope or precision correction (4g): 29 (L001, L004, L006, L015, L016, L044, L046, L054, L057, L059, L101, L105, L110, L111, L113, L114, L115, L116, L117, L125, L134, L135, L166, L136, L138, L139, L142, L149, L157). The figure passes; the note's sentence that carries it still needs a human read against 4g at the --note run, because a correction without a new numeral is invisible to the --note matcher.
- **The pass condition is not met over the provisional set**: the rows below are not found, discrepant, computed-new, not run, or carry a status outside the vocabulary. Each consequence is the block's (:23), applied to the item raw's own classification; Code does not re-decide any of them. Whether a row binds depends on whether the note carries the figure, which this run cannot read.

- **T** (8 rows): the item raw calls it transcription: block :23 corrects the note to the reproduced value (the item raw's printed value; its correction text where it gives one).
- **NT** (1 row): the item raw says it is not transcription: under block :23 that is a STOP on the item if the note carries the figure; the item raw itself declares no STOP; for the design seat and the PI.
- **NF** (3 rows): the item raw classes it as a design-seat claim or a figure label, not a record figure: a note under block :3, not an (i) discrepancy under :62 (N4's STOPs line, results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N4-e2a.md@09c3a8c:394). N4 does not address block :23. Code's reading, for the design seat (not the item raw's): block :23 reads the note's figures, and a design-seat claim or a figure label is not one, so :23's correct-or-STOP fork is not applied here. For L081 the correction is the block's own (:57: 'the post version is relabeled to the record's definition'); N4's relabel text is in the cause column.
- **SR** (4 rows): the item raw calls it a stale referent (the suite prints 154/154, not 103/103) and declares no STOP; N11's pass on N/N is Code's reading in N11, flagged there for the design seat and the PI. N1's reading of block :23 (not the item raws'): if the note carries 103/103, :23's non-transcription branch would read it as a STOP.
- **Conditional** (L053): the item raw makes the status itself conditional on what the note or figure shows (its status_condition, carried verbatim in the status cell); the T branch applies only on the discrepant side of that condition.
- **Two item raws, two statuses** (L055, L058; table 4f): L055: N3 gives 'discrepant (transcription)' (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-robustness.md@09c3a8c:385 (JSON figures[15], id ib-3)), N3c gives 'reproduced, over L51-L63' (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-compute.md@09c3a8c:311); L058: N3 gives 'discrepant (transcription)' (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-robustness.md@09c3a8c:385 (JSON figures[15], id ib-3)), N3c gives 'reproduced, over L51-L63' (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-compute.md@09c3a8c:311). The primary row is N3's: block :44 is part (i-b) of N3, and N3c's own referent split puts (i-b) in the N3 robustness raw (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-compute.md@09c3a8c:12). N3's section 11 confirms the range on L51-L63 and says it needs 'from L51' (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N3-echo-robustness.md@09c3a8c:439) without reconciling its ib-3 status with N3c's. N1 does not choose between them; the design seat decides.

## 7. --note mode

Matching rule (round 2; the round-1 matcher compared values only and is replaced). Every numeral in each file (SVG: the text content of text, tspan, title and desc elements) is split into units: a fraction k/n is one unit; otherwise each numeral, with its sign (+, -, ±, or none; a dash after a letter, digit or bracket is a range or a hyphen, not a sign), its unit kind (percent; percentage points, written 'pp' or 'points'; a multiplier attached as '×' or 'x'; else a plain number) and its decimal places. A ledger row's units are those of its span; a numeral-free row takes the units of its primary item-raw rows' figure labels. A note unit is **matched** only if a row unit has the same unit kind, the same value, a compatible sign (none = +; zero is neutral) and the same integer/decimal form, AND the row's context words (its span and its item-raw figure labels) agree with the words of the note unit's own clause (round 3: bounded by ';', a table bar, a bullet or a sentence end) within 60 characters of the unit: one agreeing word that occurs in fewer than 5 rows' contexts, or two agreeing words of any kind (a lone stage, arm or map name such as 'SFT' or 'DPO' is not enough); the agreeing words are printed. A value hit without such agreement is a **candidate (verify context)**, printed with the row's span and status. When more than one row carries the value, a match is labelled **matched; value shared by N rows (verify which)** and every other row with the value is listed as a candidate (round 3). A unit with no such value hit is **not in ledger**; it lists loose hits (the same digits or proportion with a different unit kind, sign or integer/decimal form: 0.3% against −0.3 or 0.003), rounding hits (half-up, two or more places) and item-raw figure rows carrying the value, as pointers only. Bare integers below 100 are 'weak' and never matched. Units caught by the generic rules (dates, hashes, paths, identifiers, section signs) are listed as excluded. 'Matched' is a pointer for the reader, not a verification: the context test is a word overlap.

Limits. An SVG's plotted positions (point coordinates, bar heights) are geometry, not text, and are not read: F2's plotted (FPR, TPR) points must be checked against their data file (results/raw/2026-10-08_note-N2-roc.json for ours; N2vi for the source's) by a human or by the Draftsman seat's own export.

Watch-list. Every unit whose value is one of these is surfaced in its own table, whatever it matches (sign ignored; a decimal key with stated places matches only that many places). Each entry's quotes are asserted to occur in the item-raw line cited.

## 8. Notes for the PI and the design seat (none is a STOP declared by N1)

1. Basis (updated at N1 final). Sections 1-8 are built from the block's quotations of the note; the note the block names (v0.2) was superseded by v0.3, which section 9 maps unit by unit. A block-quoted figure the note no longer carries stays in sections 2-5 as a row of the block's ledger; it binds nothing in the note. Section 9 is the ledger of the note itself.
2. Rows whose item raw gives no status in N1's vocabulary are listed in 4d with the item raw's own wording: N13 row 15 (the Q1 bet's date) uses N13's vocabulary (block :93); the N3 CV-scheme row is stated 'as stored' without a status row; the block's 'sub-0.05-cosine' wording has no N17 figure row of its own (N17's note F3 addresses it). N1 does not assign them a status.
3. Discrepant rows carry the item raws' classifications. Some item raws state that a discrepancy is a pass-condition outcome rather than a note figure and that block :23 does not attach (N2 rows 21 and 33, for (ii) and (iv)), or that it is a design-seat claim or a figure label, a note under block :3 rather than an (i) discrepancy under :62 (N4); others give a conditional (N2vi section 12; N3 ib-1's status_condition). Those statements are the item raws' and are shown in the status and cause columns. Where this ledger states what block :23 does with them (the NF and SR branch texts, the 4a conditions), it says it is Code's reading.
4. Record-source entry commits use `git log -1 <pin> -- <path>` for artifacts (the last change at or before the pin, which may be later than the commit that first wrote the figure if the file changed afterwards) and the blamed commit for line locators; first-add commits are listed beside artifacts in section 3.
5. Sources outside the record enter no entry commit; rows resting on them show it in section 3. Over the primary rows (computed from the parsed sources): data/echo/stageC_resid_s*.npz (outside the record (gitignored; local only, 102 files on this machine); rows L060, L062); jlens-qwen36/scripts/e2a_sessions.py (outside the record (gitignored clone); rows L081, L164); archive/PREPRINT-v1_0-draft.pre-patch-v2.md (outside the record (untracked (absent from any clone)); rows L071, L074); archive/PREPRINT-v1_0-draft.pre-patch-v3.md (outside the record (untracked (absent from any clone)); rows L071, L074); archive/PREPRINT-v1_0-draft.pre-patch-v4.md (outside the record (untracked (absent from any clone)); rows L071, L074); PREPRINT-v1_0-draft.md (outside the record (untracked (absent from any clone)); rows L071, L074); PREPRINT-v1_1-draft.md (outside the record (not a tracked path at the pin); rows L071).

N19 (the 0.394 and the 0.70): results/raw/ holds `results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N19-cacophony.json` (committed at `e0fd85c`, working tree equal to the committed blob; sha256 `d631aff84bd57a84`); `results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N19-cacophony.md` (committed at `e0fd85c`, working tree equal to the committed blob; sha256 `1a211f52031ef4a3`) whose status table gives 0.394 'reproduced' (:119), 0.70 'reproduced' (:120). By blame they enter at `87a1167` (results/raw/2026-10-04_m-v1.7_referent_check.txt@bbc9ffe:5196-5207) and `988e5df` (RESULTS.md@bbc9ffe:161-165); a fact enters the record with its first carrier, so section 5's third branch is `87a1167`, and the later carrier binds only if the note cites it. ### 9b. Not-figure rows, by reason

418 units are not figures. Each row carries the mapper's reason; here they are grouped by the first group whose pattern the reason contains (`NR_REASON_GROUPS`; the last group takes the rest). The full reasons are in the JSON (`note_rows_final.rows[].not_figure_reason`).

| identifier, item id, campaign or file name, path | 79 | 72 / 6 / 1 | 56 |
| axis tick label (SVG) | 51 | 0 / 33 / 18 | 11 |
| section or heading number / reference | 50 | 50 / 0 / 0 | 38 |
| link or figure identifier | 48 | 38 / 10 / 0 | 35 |
| prose: pronoun, rhetoric, editorial, or a count of the note's own parts | 45 | 44 / 1 / 0 | 42 |
| date, year or decade | 42 | 41 / 0 / 1 | 26 |
| model name, version or size label | 27 | 25 / 0 / 2 | 26 |
| list ordinal or label | 24 | 24 / 0 / 0 | 19 |
| commit-hash fragment | 22 | 22 / 0 / 0 | 9 |
| procedure or plot parameter, method name | 15 | 9 / 2 / 4 | 12 |
| citation apparatus (arXiv id, journal, cited section) | 15 | 15 / 0 / 0 | 7 |

### 9c. The figures, one row each

'status' is the row's (carried from the item raw or the ledger row; 'pass check' differs only where the ledger row's item raw gives no status in N1's vocabulary, or where a discrepant row is corrected in the note). 'corr.' names a correction recorded in 9i. 'source' is the row's first locator and its source value (cut here; whole in the JSON). 'check' = source-line level (A/B), the status check, and the ledger result. 'entry' = the commit at which the figure entered the record (record sources), and, for a figure whose only carrier is an audit raw, that raw's commit (9g).

### 9d. The block's pass check (block :23) over the note's figures

*Pass:* every figure is `reproduced` or `log-only` with a locator. *On fail:* a `not found` figure is cut from the note; a `discrepant` figure is corrected in the note to the reproduced value if the discrepancy is transcription, and is a STOP on that item if it is not. (AUDIT-note-release-v1_2.md@bbc9ffe:23)

### 9f. Corrections routed to the note, and whether the note applies them (the mappers' span notes)

Every span note, verbatim, by span (one exception: S3's redaction note, reworded in round 5 and annotated). It is mechanical: a note can say 'not done' or 'not applied' in lower case and carry no marker, so read the note. 'STOP' marks a note that uses the word, whatever it says about it. N1 final's round-5 and round-6 annotations follow the note they qualify, marked 'N1 final'; where an annotation supersedes the note, the annotation holds, and where a round-6 annotation supersedes a round-5 one, the round-6 annotation holds.

| S7 | 2 |  |  | SOFRONIEW ACTIVITY-PAIR ROW (:142 parenthesis). External, not an audit raw; arXiv:2604.07729v1 read via WebFetch 2026-10-09, twice. Told: the options are described in text ('Would you prefer to (A) {activity_A} or (B) {activity_B}?'), with an 'Assistant: (' prefill. Readout: the A and B token logits are compared after the '(' prefill and aggregated to Elo. Manipulation (§1.3): the vector is added 'on the token positions of the steered activities, while leaving the control activities unmodified'. Footnote 4: strength 0.5 relative to the average residual norm at the layer, across the middle layers. The main text does not say whether the read position (after the '(' prefill) is steered. On the paper's own description, 'manipulation on at the read' is therefore not established. The row would read: told; injection on the option spans of the same forward as the read, so the state reaches the read by propagation; read not insulated. That is structurally like the lab's ledger 5(f) class (memory-present, state-absent-at-read). Residual uncertainty, unchanged after this pass: §1.3 defers steering details to Appendix 6.10. The arXiv HTML text ends inside Appendix 6.5 (all three 100k-char segments read; 6.10 absent), the PDF exceeded the fetch size limit, and the transformer-circuits.pub page exceeded it in the first pass. A person should read Appendix 6.10 before the hedge is changed; until then the hedge stays, as the note has it. The preprint's abbreviated table (PREPRINT-v1_0-draft.md:208-219, untracked) has no Sofroniew row; the only tracked mention is one line in PROPOSAL-g. |
| S7 | 3 |  |  | TAGLIABUE PARAGRAPH (:142) AGAINST THE SOURCE'S VERSIONS (WebFetch 2026-10-09, external). arXiv:2609.16247v1 (2026-09-14) §4.3 supports every claim in the sentence. It gives 57.7% vs 79.7% for the 32B, the 7B reversed (72.1% vs 62.6%) and a small gap for the 72B (58.7% vs 62.1%). Its arms are A pain+working, B pain+fake, C random+working and D unsteered, with no random+fake arm. The models were LoRA-tuned to remove baseline self-denial (1,684 pairs, 3 epochs). The current v2 (2026-09-25, retitled '...Act on It') differs. (a) Its text does not contain the strings 57.7/79.7/72.1/58.7; Appendix A's tables are images and were not read. (b) It reports a random-direction sham comparison: 'sham-minus-real gap of +58 points under pain and +52 under random' (32B). It concludes that the re-press gap tracks the steering state, not the described relief. (c) Its unlabeled off-switch tests find no preferential removal of pain. So the note's 'none with a fake one' is out of date against v2. The first of 'the two arms' has in part been run by the authors, with the outcome the note predicts. The note should cite v1 explicitly or update to v2. These figures have no record source, so their status is 'not found': block :23 cuts them unless the PI admits an external citation. |

### 9g. The commit at which the last of the note's figures entered the record (the tag commit)

Rule, per figure (not-found figures enter nothing): a figure mapped to a ledger row takes that row's entry from sections 2-5; a figure mapped to record lines takes, per record locator, the latest blamed commit of its lines, and over its locators the earliest (a fact enters with its first carrier); a figure mapped to an item-raw figure row takes that row's record sources; a computed-new figure (by its own status, its ledger row's or its item-raw row's; or, where no record entry is found, by the row's source value or the item-raw line it cites) enters with the audit raw: the blamed commit of the first audit-raw line it cites; and (round 5) a figure the audit reproduced from an external source, whose only carrier is an audit raw committed after the audit pin (N19's), enters with that raw. Over figures, the latest. Each figure's entry and how it was found are in the JSON (`note_rows_final.entries`).

- **Over record sources only:** `05c4636` (2026-10-06T00:42:09-04:00), set by S6#91, S6#92, S9#85.
- **Counting a ledger-mapped figure at its first record carrier where that is later than its sources** (section 5's third branch; the note carries the f1 CORR dose caveat as a statement at :111): `05c4636` (2026-10-06T00:42:09-04:00), set by S6#91, S6#92, S9#85. Figures with such a carrier: S1#64 (40/306, note:3): sources 50906bf, first carrier 87a1167; S4#31 (.10, note:86): sources d04cd91, first carrier e9b5022; S6#29 (83.4%, note:108): sources d49bb5d, first carrier db5b09c; S6#58 (40, note:111): sources 50906bf, first carrier 87a1167; S6#59 (306, note:111): sources 50906bf, first carrier 87a1167; S6#60 (16, note:111): sources 50906bf, first carrier 87a1167; S6#61 (56, note:111): sources 50906bf, first carrier 87a1167; S6#62 (one, note:111): sources 50906bf, first carrier 87a1167; S6#63 (93%, note:111): sources 50906bf, first carrier c07a5e0; S6#65 (344, note:111): sources 50906bf, first carrier c07a5e0; S6#66 (344, note:111): sources 50906bf, first carrier c07a5e0; S6#67 (zero, note:111): sources 50906bf, first carrier c07a5e0; S7#46 (83.4%, note:156): sources d49bb5d, first carrier db5b09c. It does not move the candidate.
- **Including the audit raws** (the note carries 62 computed-new figures and 4 figures whose only carrier is N19's raw): `e0fd85c` (2026-10-09T13:35:17-04:00), set by 5 figures (S1#145, S7#29, S7#33, S8#84, S8#85).
- Figures entering with N19's raw (round 5): S7#29 (0.394, note:144) at `e0fd85c` (results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N19-cacophony.md@e0fd85c:119); S7#33 (0.70, note:144) at `e0fd85c` (results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N19-cacophony.md@e0fd85c:120); S8#84 (0.394, note:210) at `e0fd85c` (results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N19-cacophony.md@e0fd85c:119); S8#85 (0.70, note:210) at `e0fd85c` (results/raw/2026-10-09_note-N19-cacophony.md@e0fd85c:120).

### 9i. Corrections recorded separately from status (round 5)

A correction is what the note's sentence should say, recorded beside the row's carried status, never as a status (10b). 'whose' says whether the reading is an item raw's or N1's own. Kind: wording, precision or scope. Since round 6 the script declares every correction, with its kind and whose reading it is (NR_CORRECTIONS, 11b); the rows here are exactly those.

## 10. Values check, round 5 (N1 final fixes, 2026-10-09)

An adversarial check of round 4 (the final checker: a re-run, a 50-row sample against the sources, 29 mutations of the rows file and a status cross-check over the 413 figures) found no value error and confirmed the tag result at 09c3a8c; it raised the nine findings below. The PI's routing of 2026-10-09 adds item 10. Each finding is Code's summary of the checker's; each disposition says what this round changed. The changes are this script's (the validator, the guard, the sections that render them) and the rows file's (named rows, each with a 'round5' record).

### 10b. Rules adopted this round

- **Status (finding 1).** A row's status is its item raw's (or ledger row's), carried exactly; N1 assigns none of its own. A wording, scope or precision problem N1 reads in the note's sentence is recorded in the row's 'correction' ({kind, text, whose, basis}, 9i), never as a status. 'whose' says whether the reading is an item raw's or N1's own.
- **Status check (finding 2).** item-raw and audit-raw-new rows: the first cited item-raw line that gives a status decides (its JSON figure row's status, matched by exact line text at the audit pin; else the cell under its table's 'status' column, including N11's text table); where none does, the row's status_basis (a line locator and a quote asserted on it, whose line gives a status or whose quote carries exactly one term); else an audit-raw-new row is computed-new by its mapping and an item-raw row fails. record rows: log-only, unless the row's first line locator is an item-raw line that gives a status. ledger-row rows: the ledger row's status, as before. No note exempts a row from this check. (Amended in round 6, 11b: record rows read every cited item-raw line, '[status: …]' tags are read, a status_basis must be in a file the row cites, and every row that names a ledger row carries its status.)
- **Double rounding (finding 4).** A note figure that rounds from a printed value which itself rounds a more precise value the row states, while the precise value rounds to something else at the note's places, is a double rounding. The item raw's status is carried; the row must carry a precision correction (else the run fails); the form the note uses is the PI's choice.
- **Corrected in the note (finding 6).** A row whose item raw is discrepant and whose note figure now carries the item raw's correction keeps the status 'discrepant', is marked corrected_in_note ({locator, quote}: a line the row cites, quoting the item raw's correction, which states the note's value), and counts as passing when the note's figure ties to the source value at level A. A mark that fails any of these fails the run. (Amended in round 6, 11b.)
- **Value check (finding 2).** Level A: a cited line carries a value the source_value states that is tied to the note's number (equal, or stated to more places and rounding half-up to it; a fraction k/n, written 'k/n' or 'k of n', is also its exact value; a fraction unit needs the same k/n in the source_value and on the line). Level B: a cited line carries another distinctive value the row states, never a year (1900-2099), a cited line number, a leading-zero identifier, a section number ('§7.3'), a version inside a hyphenated name ('OLMo-3.1-32B'), or 0/1. A decimal or fraction unit must pass at level A.
- **Stated values (finding 2).** Every value a source_value states to two or more places, and every fraction it states ('k/n' or 'k of n', not '1/2'), is on a line the row cites (its locator's lines, and line references inside the source_value, read against the locator's first file when they name none). A misstated value fails even when another stated value ties the note's number.
- **Ledger span (finding 2).** A ledger id a row names must carry the note's number: the ledger row's block span, or its item-raw rows' figure labels or printed values, contain the unit's text, a value tied to it (fractions as values) or its number word.
- **Kind (finding 2).** The unit -> kind assignment (one line per row: span#seq, file, line, unit, kind) hashes to NR_KIND_SHA256 in the script; any other assignment stops the run, so a reclassification is a visible change to the script.
- **Exemptions (finding 2).** A failure is explained only by 'validation_exempts' mapping its check (locator-value, source-values, ledger-status or ledger-span) to a substring of its message, with a validation_note saying why; a locator-value exemption also needs a validation_quote on the row's first cited line. An exemption that explains nothing fails the run. (Amended in round 6, 11b: the whole message, and a fingerprint the script declares.)

### 10d. Mutation self-test (run in memory at every run; the rows file is never changed)

Each mutation changes the rows data in memory and the validation runs again; it is caught when a failure no named exemption explains is reported, or when the run stops (a frozen hash, coverage, or an assertion on a row the script names). A mutation that is not caught, or a known limit that is caught, stops the run before anything is written. At this run: **121 of 121** fixed mutations caught (29 checker, 26 round 5, 27 checker 2, 11 round 6, 20 checker 3, 8 round 7); **123 of 123** family members caught (0 not applicable); **2 of 2** known limits uncaught by a whole run, and **3 of 3** row-level limits uncaught by the row checks and caught by the status freeze, as 10f says. 'checker' = the final checker's 29 (round 5); 'checker 2' = n1chk2's that round 5 did not catch, or caught only through this self-test's coupling (round 6); 'checker 3' = r6chk's that round 6 did not catch (round 7); 'round 7' = Code's. Round 7's families F-H, evaluated row by row, are in 12d. The last column says whether the row checks alone catch the mutation (round 7; '-' where it changed no row).

| id | origin | mutation | caught by (the first failure) | row checks |
|---|---|---|---|---|

### 10f. What the validator still does not catch

Current at this run: round 7 rewrote bullets 3, 5 and 6, extended 4, and added 7 and 8 (12a).

- Integer and word units can still pass at level B on another distinctive value the row states that is on a cited line (26 rows at level B). Since round 6 an integer unit's own number must be stated in the source_value (the unit-stated check), but the cited line need not carry it. The source-values check covers every decimal (one place or more) and every fraction a source_value states; an integer elsewhere in a source_value, neither the unit nor in a fraction, is not checked (self-test L03).
- A word unit ('one', 'two') passes at level A when the word occurs on a cited line, whatever its source_value says (self-test L02); the word can be a coincidence. S7#12's round-4 pass and S7#15's round-5 pass were; both rows now carry a locator-value exemption that says what their lines do carry.
- Status binding (rewritten in round 7). The status check binds a row's status to the cited item-raw lines that give a status and carry a value the row states, to its quoted status_basis (an item-raw line; a single-term clause where its line gives several terms), or to the ledger row it names, which the ledger-bind check ties to the row. The row checks cannot tell a row's own figure line from another figure's line that carries the same value, or the clause that applies from another clause of the same line. So a status changed together with one more field still passes them: a locator prefix with a reproduced figure row that carries one of the row's values (67 of the 184 rows whose status is computed-new, discrepant, not found or log-only; family G, 12d), a ledger_row swap to a row the locator binds (1 of 184; family H), a status_basis moved with the status to another line or clause of a cited item raw (L01; N3c :151's 'reproduced' clause, L04), and a 'related row' field cut to its ledger row (L05). Since round 7 every such change also changes the input of the status freeze (NR_STATUS_SHA256: every row's maps_to, status, ledger_row and status_basis), so the run fails (status-hash) unless the script is changed with it, where it shows in a diff. The freeze does not judge whether a status is right.
- The frozen declarations (NR_KIND_SHA256, NR_CLAUSE_SHA256, NR_STATUS_SHA256, NR_TEXT_SHA256, NR_EXEMPT_FP, NR_CORRECTIONS with its fingerprints, NR_QUOTE_REVIEWED, NR_DISC, the watch rows and the open items' rows) freeze; they do not judge. A row edited together with its declaration passes, and both show in a diff. Whether a unit is a figure, whether a clause is a fair cut of its sentence, and whether a status, a correction or a comment is right stays the mappers' and their checkers' reading.
- Ledger rows (rewritten in round 7). The ledger-span check accepts any named ledger row that carries the value, so a common value (0.000, 0.5) can pass on a wrong row. Since round 7 the ledger-bind check requires the row's first ledger id to be named in its locator, or one of that ledger row's item-raw lines to be cited, and every ledger id the locator names to be in ledger_row; a swap to a ledger row the locator binds passes the row checks (bullet 3). Every ledger_row change, of any status, fails the status freeze.
- Free text is frozen, not judged (rewritten in round 7). NR_TEXT_SHA256 covers every comment, cause, not-figure reason and validation note, every change record (round5, round6 and round7, their 'why' and 'previous' included), every span note and annotation, and the file's how_built, schema and provenance; NR_CORRECTIONS' fingerprints cover each correction's text and basis (an N1-own correction's basis line is bound to the declaration, not checked against the correction). Beyond that, only the quotes the script asserts (NR_DISC's causes, the open items' and the watch-list's span notes) and the quotation scan read it.
- Not-found rows (round 7). A not-found row's locator and source value are in the status freeze, and every file and line they cite must exist (pins); whether the search they describe was complete stays the mappers' reading.
- The quotation scan (round 7) is a heuristic: a quoted span of fifteen words or more within reach of a name in NR_QUOTE_EXT_RE, in the rows file only. A long quotation without quotation marks, or with no such name nearby, is not flagged, and the script's own prose and the rendered ledger are not scanned; the rule on external quotations stays the author's to keep.
- A mutation test run from outside the script that edits a field one of the self-test's own mutations edits makes that self-test mutation not applicable, and the run stops for that reason, not by validation (n1chk2's E05 and C18 did). The self-test now runs both, and their decoupled twins, each on a fresh copy.
- Every check is a consistency check on the mapping; none re-derives a figure.

Determinism (two runs compared) and the redaction check are run by Code on this script's outputs, outside the script; their results are reported with the commit, not here.

## 11. Values check, round 6 (N1 final fixes, round 2, 2026-10-09)

An adversarial check of round 5 (n1chk2: a re-run, the provisional mode against 6485da2, a 30-row sample against the sources, 59 mutations each run as its own process, a quotation scan and a redaction check) found no value error in the sample and confirmed both tag candidates; it found the validator weak (24 of its mutations passed) and raised the nine findings below. Each finding is Code's summary of the checker's; each disposition says what this round changed. The self-test (10d) runs every one of the checker's mutations that round 5 missed; the known limits that remain are in 10f.

### 11a. Findings and dispositions

| # | severity | finding (Code's summary) | disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MEDIUM-HIGH | maps_to was never checked: a computed-new row remapped to 'record' with status 'log-only' passed end to end (29 of 62 at the row checks, 25 after the script's hard-coded lists). The record rule fell back to log-only when the first cited item-raw line gave no status; nothing required a record row to cite a record line; N3c's '[status: …]' tags were not read; and a ledger-row row remapped to 'record' skipped the ledger-status comparison. 10f did not say so. | APPLIED. A maps-to check: item-raw and audit-raw-new rows must cite an item-raw line, and record rows a record line (outside the audit outputs, at or before bbc9ffe). The record rule reads every cited item-raw line that gives a status, and N3c's inline '[status: …]' tags are read. A row that names a ledger row carries that row's status whatever its maps_to. Five rows differ for a stated reason and carry ledger-status exemptions: the four N19 rows against the provisional ledger's 'not run' (L159, L160) and S7#73 against L088's stale referent. All 62 computed-new rows remapped to record and log-only now fail (self-test family A). Every one fails maps-to, and 52 also fail status or ledger-status. (Round 7, 12a finding 1: the class was not closed. A one-field change from computed-new to reproduced still passed on five rows, through N3c :151's two-term tag and the 'related row' fields.) |
| 2 | MEDIUM | The corrected_in_note check verified nothing that mattered. All 19 uncorrected discrepant rows passed with a mark quoting the note's own number from their own cited line, and then counted as passing. On S6#68 the ledger said both 'passes (corrected in the note)' and that O2 is a live STOP. 10f did not say so. | APPLIED, all three of the checker's fixes, the third in adjusted form. The quote must carry the item raw's correction wording ('correct to', 'corrected to', 'Corrected value:') followed by the note's value. The marked rows must be exactly NR_DISC's branch C, or the run stops. The cited line must be the item raw's JSON figure row, and the note's value must differ from the discrepant figure that row quotes: for S1#73 and S5#38, N2 :51 quotes '+0.096' and the note carries 0.095. The checker asked for a difference from the item raw's printed value. N2's printed value is the reproduced one, which a corrected note carries by design, so the check compares the quoted figure instead. All 19 forged marks now fail (family B), and so do C05-C07 and C20. S6#68 is branch NT, so the contradiction cannot recur. |
| 3 | MEDIUM (hard rule) | A 20-word verbatim quotation of arXiv:2609.16247v1 was still in the files. Round 5 cut it from the S7#19/S7#20 comments but kept it in round5.changed[].previous: twice in the rows file and four times in the final JSON. | APPLIED. The quotation is removed from both rows' change records and replaced by a bracketed statement that a twenty-word quotation was removed. Round 6's own record names the change without repeating the quotation. Code re-ran n1chk2's quotation scan (quoted spans of fifteen or more words near an external citation, outside the script) on the round-5 and the round-6 files: every hit new in round 6 is round 6's own prose, none a quotation of an external source (n1chk2 had found every other external quotation at fourteen words or fewer), and neither half of the removed sentence occurs in any of the four files. |
| 4 | LOW-MEDIUM | Exemptions were matched by substring, not by the exact message, so a forged 'FAIL' exempted anything (E05b). The five locator-value exemptions used the generic message, so their source_value could be anything (E01-E04). Round 5's summary ('matched to the exact failure message') overclaimed. | APPLIED. An exemption explains a failure only when it equals the whole message, and the locator-value message names the values sought and the lines read. Every exempted row is declared in the script with a fingerprint of its mapping fields (NR_EXEMPT_FP, 18 rows), so a change to any of them leaves the failure unexplained. E01-E06 and E05b now fail, and so does every exemption cut to half its message (family E). 10a's text for round 5's finding 2 now says that round 5 matched a substring. |
| 5 | LOW | Corrections could be deleted (C08-C11) or misattributed (C21) without failing the run; their 9i entries disappeared while the hard-coded O-items survived. | APPLIED. NR_CORRECTIONS declares the twelve corrections: row, kind, and whose reading it is (N1's own, or an item raw's, whose basis must then cite that raw). A missing, undeclared, re-kinded or re-attributed correction fails. The open items that rest on a correction (O1, O4, O5, O9, O17) name it, and each is asserted. C08-C11 and C21 now fail, and so do families C and D (every correction removed; every 'whose' swapped). |
| 6 | LOW | O5's 'Not a STOP' overstated N6. N6 @09c3a8c:505 says that whether these rows are figure STOPs or sentence corrections is N1's and the design seat's call, not N6's; N6's own reading is a sentence correction. The O5 headline and the S2/S7 annotations dropped the design seat. | APPLIED. O5 now says what N6 says. N6's gloss row is 'discrepant' (a definition error, not a transcription; no number is discrepant, :493). N6 raises no STOP and leaves the call to N1 and the design seat (:505). N1 reads the row as a sentence correction, as N6 does, and records it on S2#37, S2#38 and S7#61. The design seat rules; if it does not accept that reading, block :23's non-transcription branch makes it a STOP on N6. The S2 and S7 span notes carry round-6 annotations that supersede round 5's, and the S2 mapper's conditional framing stands. The 9h preamble lists O5 as conditional. |
| 7 | LOW | Two fields were unchecked: a locator revision moved off the pins passed (C15), and so did an altered clause (C16). | APPLIED. A pins check requires every revision a row cites (locator, source value, status basis, corrected_in_note, correction basis) to be its path class's pin. The clauses are frozen by a hash (NR_CLAUSE_SHA256), like the kind assignment. 98 of the 504 clauses are not verbatim on their note line (the mappers describe SVG nodes and mark range ends), so a text check would not hold; the hash freezes the clauses and does not judge them. C15 and C16 now fail. |
| 8 | LOW (disclosed) | Confirmed as disclosed in 10f: integers still pass at level B (C12, C13), one-place decimals are unchecked (C14), and a status changed together with its basis passes (C18b). Round 5's summary 'every stated decimal' left out 'two or more places'. | PARTLY CLOSED; the rest stays disclosed (10f). An integer unit's number must now be stated in the source value (tied, a floor such as 17.500 for '17×', a label such as 'L60', or a percentage), so C12 and C13 fail. One-place decimals are checked, so C14 fails; S5#48's '97.5' needed its table's column header (N2 :399) cited, and 'section 5.6' is now stripped whole. A status_basis in a file the row does not cite now fails; C18 and C18b moved the basis within N3c, the file they cite, and fail instead on the status tag (C18) and the ledger check (both). Still open, each run in the self-test as a known limit that must stay uncaught: a status and its basis moved together within one item raw (L01); a word unit whose word is on a cited line, with any source value (L02); and an integer elsewhere in a source value (L03). 10a's text for round 5's finding 2 now says 'two or more places'. |
| 9 | nits | O4 lists S8#78, but 9i records no correction on it (S8#79 has one). S7#15's 'second' passed at level A on an unrelated 'second' (PROPOSAL-m :174). | APPLIED. O4 and the d′ watch line now say that the correction is on S1#26, S1#27 and S8#79, because S8 records :208's range once, on its upper end; the three are asserted. S7#15's :174 and :186 move from its locator to the source value's context; they support 'the scaffolded cell', not the count. The row carries a locator-value exemption: the ordinal is the S7 mapper's count of the record's armed clauses, and no line states it. |

### 11b. Rules adopted or amended this round

- **maps-to (finding 1).** item-raw and audit-raw-new rows cite at least one item-raw line (an item raw of 2026-10-08, or N19's of 2026-10-09); record rows cite at least one record line (a path outside the audit outputs, at or before bbc9ffe, readable there). Not exemptable.
- **Status check (finding 1; amends 10b).** record rows: every cited item-raw line that gives a status is read, and the row's status is one of their terms (log-only where none gives one). An inline '[status: …]' tag on a cited line gives its terms (several terms: any of them). A status_basis is a line of a file the row's locator cites. A row that names a ledger row (not 'related only') carries that row's status whatever its maps_to; a row whose status differs for a stated reason carries a ledger-status exemption. (Amended in round 7, 12b: several terms decide nothing; a status line decides only where it carries the row's value; a 'related row' field is compared with that related row only.)
- **Corrected in the note (finding 2; amends 10b).** The quote carries the item raw's correction wording ('correct to', 'corrected to', 'Corrected value:') followed by the note's value; the line is the item raw's JSON figure row; the note's value differs from the discrepant figure that row quotes; and the rows marked are exactly NR_DISC's branch C (else the run stops).
- **Exemptions (finding 4; amends 10b).** An exemption explains a failure only when it equals the whole message; the locator-value message names the values sought and the lines read. Every exempted row is declared in NR_EXEMPT_FP with a fingerprint (the first 16 hex of a sha256) over its note file and line, unit, kind, maps_to, status, ledger row, locator, source value, status basis, correction, corrected_in_note, exemptions, quote and note; any change to them, or an undeclared exemption, fails. Exemptable: locator-value, source-values, ledger-status, ledger-span, unit-stated.
- **Corrections (finding 5).** NR_CORRECTIONS declares each correction: its row, kind, and whose reading it is (N1's own: 'whose' starts "N1's"; an item raw's: 'whose' starts '<item> (' and the basis cites that raw). A missing, undeclared, re-kinded or re-attributed correction fails; an open item that rests on a correction names it, asserted.
- **Pins and clauses (finding 7).** Every revision a row cites is its path class's pin: the record at bbc9ffe; item raws at 09c3a8c (N8 and N11 also at 02b6a61); N19's raw at e0fd85c; the provisional ledger at 6485da2. The clauses hash to NR_CLAUSE_SHA256 ('span#seq<TAB>clause', file order).
- **Unit stated; one-place decimals (finding 8).** An integer unit's number is stated in the source_value: a tied numeral, a value whose integer part it is (a floor), the unit's digits inside a label ('L60', '4bit'), or, where the note prints it as a percentage, a proportion tied at the percentage scale. The source-values check covers one-place decimals (equal on a cited line). A dotted section number ('section 5.6') is stripped whole.
- **Self-test (all findings).** Besides round 5's 55, the self-test runs the 27 n1chk2 mutations round 5 did not catch, or caught only through the self-test's own coupling ('checker 2'), and Code's 11 ('round 6'). It also runs five families generated from the rows: (A) every computed-new row remapped to record and log-only; (B) every uncorrected discrepant row given a forged corrected_in_note; (C) every correction removed; (D) every correction's 'whose' swapped; (E) every exemption cut to half its message. Three known limits must stay uncaught (10f). A mutation that is not caught, or a limit that is caught, stops the run.

## 12. Values check, round 7 (N1 final fixes, round 3, 2026-10-09)

A strict check of round 6 (r6chk: 64 mutations, each run as its own process on a scratch copy of the rows file; an in-memory screen of status upgrades over every figure row; a re-run, the provisional mode against 6485da2, a 30-row sample against the sources, a quotation scan and a redaction check) found no value error in the sample and confirmed determinism and the section 10f/11 counts. It returned FAIL on two substantive findings (status upgrades the validator did not catch and 10f/11 did not disclose) and raised two minor ones. Each finding is Code's summary of the checker's; each disposition says what this round changed.

### 12a. Findings and dispositions

| # | severity | finding (Code's summary) | disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | substantive | A one-field edit changed a computed-new figure to reproduced and passed end to end on five rows, and 10f and 11 did not disclose it; 11a reported round 6's finding 1 as closed. Two causes: the 'several terms: any of them' rule for '[status: …]' tags let N3c :151's tag (computed-new, except one column reproduced) pass 'reproduced' for S4#23 and S7#51; and a ledger_row naming a related row ('L004 (related row: N2 row 37, computed-new)') also accepted the primary row's status, so S5#20, S5#22 and S5#23 passed as reproduced. | APPLIED. (a) A source that gives several terms (a '[status: …]' tag, a status cell, a JSON 'mixed' status, or a record row's lines together) decides nothing by itself: the row needs a status_basis whose quote is the single-term clause that applies, and the quote's term decides. S4#23 and S7#51 already quote N3c :151's computed-new clause, which now decides. (b) A ledger_row that names a related row is compared with that related row only (resolved by item and JSON index; the status the field states must be the related row's), and a 'related only' field leaves the status to the row's own check. A new self-test family F sets every figure row's status to each other term, one field at a time: the row checks alone catch every member (12d); r6chk's N08-N11 and N32 are in the self-test, each caught by the row checks. What remains, a clause or basis moved with the status (L04, L01), is in 10f bullet 3. 11a's finding 1 now points here. |
| 2 | substantive | Two-field upgrades passed, and 10f's disclosures were narrower than the hole. The status check bound a row to whatever item-raw line it cited first, or to whatever ledger row it named; nothing checked that the line or ledger row addresses the row's figure. A status change plus an unrelated locator line, a swapped ledger_row, or a status_basis on an unrelated record line passed (N04-N07, N12-N15). The checker's screen found 44 of the 62 computed-new rows and 75 of the 88 log-only rows upgradable with at most two fields. | APPLIED, in two layers. Row checks: a cited item-raw status line decides only where it carries a value the row states (the locator check, read on that line), so another figure's line decides nothing; a status_basis must be an item-raw line, and every cited status line that carries the row's value must agree with it; a new ledger-bind check requires the first ledger id to be named in the locator, or one of its item-raw rows' lines to be cited, and every ledger id the locator names to be in ledger_row. Fourteen locators now name their ledger row, and S2#34, S5#61 and S10#62 name their comparison rows (12c). The checker's level-A option for the deciding line was tried: it broke five rows and closed 10 of the 67 rows Code's screen found open, so level B counts where the locator check accepts it. Freeze: NR_STATUS_SHA256 over every row's maps_to, status, ledger_row and status_basis, the checker's simplest robust option, so every status or ledger change is also a script diff. N04-N07, N12 and N13 now fail the row checks; N14 and N15 fail only the freeze (N2 :24 carries a value S5#35 and S3#7 state). Families G (a reproduced figure row prefixed) and H (a ledger row swapped) run over the 184 rows whose status is computed-new, discrepant, not found or log-only: every member is caught, and 12d counts those the row checks let through. 10f bullets 3 and 5 now say what remains. S10#68, the checker's illustration, carries a status_basis on N2 :136, its own inline status; S9#57 carries one on N2 :90, whose status cell no longer decides without it. |
| 3 | minor | Not-found rows accepted fabricated locators and source values, including nonexistent files and lines (N16, N17); the pins check verified only the revision, and the ledger rendered what it was given. | APPLIED. The pins check also requires every cited file to exist at its revision and every cited line to be within it, on every row. S4#28 and S4#32 named 'stageC_analysis.json@bbc9ffe', which is no file at that revision; they now give the stored analyses' full paths under data/echo/. A not-found row's locator and source value are in the status freeze. N16 now fails status-hash and N17 fails pins. Whether a not-found row's search was complete stays the mappers' reading (10f bullet 7). |
| 4 | minor | Two fields were unchecked, and 10f's free-text list did not name them: the change records ('previous', 'why'; where round 5's twenty-word quotation survived; N28), and the basis of an N1-own correction (N27). | APPLIED, beyond naming them. NR_TEXT_SHA256 freezes the free text and the change history: every comment, cause, not-figure reason, validation note, change record, span note and annotation, and the file's how_built, schema and provenance. N20, N28, N29 and N30 now fail text-hash. NR_CORRECTIONS carries a fingerprint of each whole correction (kind, text, whose, basis), so N27 fails correction-declared. The optional scan runs at every run: quoted spans of fifteen words or more near the name of an external source, in every string of the rows file. Its two hits (S1#25 and S7#16 quote item-raw lines) are declared with Code's reading, and an undeclared hit fails validation (T06 plants one). 10f bullets 6 and 8 say what stays open: frozen text is not judged, and the scan is a heuristic. |

### 12b. Rules adopted or amended this round

- **Status check (findings 1 and 2; amends 10b and 11b).** A cited item-raw line that gives a status decides only where it carries a value the row states (nr_line_carries: the locator check read on that line alone, level A, or B where B is accepted); a status line that carries none decides nothing. A source with several terms (a '[status: …]' tag, a status cell, a JSON 'mixed' status, a record row's lines together) decides nothing by itself. A status_basis is a line of an item raw the row cites (never a record line); its line's single term decides, or, where the line gives several terms or none, its quote's single term (one of the line's where it gives several); every cited status line that carries the row's value must give the basis's term too. Without a basis: item-raw and audit-raw-new rows take the first such line's term (an audit-raw-new row with none is computed-new; an item-raw row with none fails), record rows their lines' single term, or log-only.
- **ledger-status (finding 1).** 'Lnnn (related row: <item> row <n>, <status>)' compares the row's status with that related row only, resolved by item and JSON index n-1, and the stated status must be the related row's; '(related only …)' leaves the status to the row's own check (the ledger row is context).
- **ledger-bind (finding 2).** The first ledger id a row names is named in its locator, or the locator cites a line of one of that ledger row's item-raw rows (same path and line number, the same text at both revisions; the named related row for 'related row', any for 'related only'); and every ledger id the locator names is in ledger_row. Not exemptable.
- **Pins (finding 3; amends 11b).** Every cited file exists at its revision and every cited line is within it, on every row and in every field the pins check reads.
- **Freezes (findings 2-4).** NR_STATUS_SHA256: one line per row, 'span#seq, maps_to, status, ledger_row, status_basis', and for a not-found row its locator and source value. NR_TEXT_SHA256: the file's item, title, how_built, schema and provenance, every span, and per row its comment, cause, not_figure_reason, validation_note and change records. NR_CORRECTIONS: a fingerprint of each whole correction. A mismatch fails validation; a deliberate change is made in the script too.
- **Quotation scan (finding 4).** Every string of the rows file is scanned for quoted spans of fifteen words or more within 250 characters before or 120 after a name in NR_QUOTE_EXT_RE (the round-6 checker's list); each hit is declared in NR_QUOTE_REVIEWED by its sha256 prefix with Code's reading, and an undeclared hit fails validation.
- **Self-test (all findings).** Besides round 6's, the self-test runs r6chk's twenty mutations that round 6 did not catch ('checker 3'), Code's eight ('round 7'), three families evaluated row by row (F: every figure row's status set to each other term, expected caught by the row checks alone; G: a reproduced figure row prefixed to the locator, and H: a ledger row swapped, each with the status 'reproduced', over every row whose status is computed-new, discrepant, not found or log-only, expected caught; G and H pick the first candidate the row checks let through, so they report the worst case), and five known limits at two levels: 'full' (L02, L03; uncaught by a whole run) and 'row' (L01, L04, L05; uncaught by the row checks, caught by the status freeze). Each self-test entry also says whether the row checks alone catch it.

Each member mutates one row; the row checks run on that row alone and the status freeze compares the row's freeze line, which is exactly what a whole run's validation reports for a one-row mutation.
